Bruce Bloomingdale, Troy School District resident,  addressed 
the Board regarding a change in the format agenda for Board 
of Education meetings.  He suggested that meeting agendas 
contain a rep ort from employee representatives for each 
group of employees.  Mr. Bloomingdale also mentioned that 
he had attended the recent informational meetings held by 
the District and complimented the Board and administration 
on a job well done.  He said that the budget challenges 
facing the District have not been caused by anyone in 
particular and that it will take everyone working together to 
overcome these challenges.

Student Congress will be attending a state conference and

Andrew Michail, student representative from Troy High 
School, reported that Troy High will be conducting a charity 
event called “March Stach eness.”  This event requires 
teachers to grow mustaches and students will vote for their 
favorite mustache  by placing money in bins during lunch 
hour according to their favorite mustache .  The money 
collected will go to Camp Make -A-Dream which is a camp 
designed for people of all ages who are affected by cancer.

                      Library Automation Software                      
Mr. S. Bryan, Director of Technology, has requested a purchase of new library 
automation software for district-wide usage.  The bid for this product has been 
completed through Oakland Schools. (This bid meets all of our criteria for a sealed 
competitive bid).  Mr. Bryan’s attached memo further documents the rationale for this 
purchase.

the following purchase be approved:  
   Follett Software Co.  $ 62,601.00 
Please include this as an action item on the April 20, 2010 Board Agenda as an 
expenditure of the 2010/11 General Fund.

          RE:      Bid Award -

Barbara A. Fowler, Ph.D., Superintendent of Schools 
Technology Resource Center  Scott Bryan, Director of Technology  
4420 Livernois Phone No. 248-823-5090

MEMORANDUM 
To: Mike Adamczyk, Assistant Superintendent, Business Services  
From: Scott Bryan, Director of Technology  
Re: Library Automation software 
Date:  April 1, 2010 - REVISED 
Cc: Barbara Fowler, Superintendent  
Per our conversation of March 3, please add a line item to the 2010 -11 operational budget for Library 
automation conversion.  We will be converting from self -hosted SirsiDynix “Horizon” to Follett 
“Destiny” hosted by Oakland Schools this summer, purchasing from the Oakland Schools Library 
Automation bid that extends to all county districts (Oakland Schools Bid #9.0041).  Frank Lams has 
reviewed the Oakland Schools bid docum ent and confirms that it complies with district policy.  The 
total one year cost for the conversion is $62,601.  As you will see below, part of this expense is 
covered by an already budgeted line item, so the additional budget request for 2010 -11 is $62,601 - 
$12,074, or $50,527. 
Our current contract with SirsiDynix renewed February 1, 2010 for a total of $12,073.54 and runs until 
January 31, 2011.  In the meantime, we are scheduled to begin our conversion to Follett over the 
summer of 2010, to be fully li ve by September 30, 2010.  The annual maintenance for the new Follett 
solution is slightly higher, at $13,402.03, which will not be due for the first year of operation, i.e., after 
July of 2011 pending final execution of an agreement between Troy School Di strict and Follett. 
I am recommending that we proceed with this schedule, despite the fact that we will be licensed for the 
SirsiDynix product for a few months after the Follett system is live.  I have inquired with SirsiDynix 
about a month-to-month conversion schedule in order to redirect some of the maintenance costs to the 
one-time conversion expense.  However, the company indicates that their terms are only year by year, 
and there is value in having access to the legacy system for a few months after a conversion.   
On the next page is a cost analysis demonstrating the need to make this conversion.  An additional 
factor that is difficult to quantify monetarily is that converting to an Oakland Schools hosted solution 
will help satisfy the State’s impending directive to conso lidate services among local school districts.  
While I cannot put a dollar figure to this, certainly proactive steps in this direction are worthwhile.  
  An Equal Opportunity/Affirmative Action Employer  
102
Cost Analysis for Library Automation conversion  
(italics = one-time or extended cost, standard print = annual recurring) 
Description Current $ Future $ Savings Notes 
One time cost of conversion $0  $62,601  ($62,601)   
One time server upgrades $2,500  $0  $2,500  bondable 
2011 server replacement, 5 yr cycle $12,000  $0  $12,000  bondable? 
2010-11 budgeted annual maintenance $12,073.54  $0  $12,074    
Salary + benefits for media tech $43,181  $0  $43,181  Note 1 
Annual maintenance $12,073.54  $13,402.03  ($1,328)  
    Subtotal $5,825    
Potential first year savings $5,825        
First year cost w/ tech staffed ($48,027)      
Annual savings after staffing adjustment $41,853        
First year cost w/ tech staffed = cost of conversion – one time server upgrades – annual maint 
Annual savings = salary + benefits – increase in annual maintenance 
Note 1: The current Media Technician has a total budget impact of $54,309 including benefits; she is 
at the top of the salary scale for a Level V clerical position, plus receives a longevity bonus per 
contract.  The calculation above shows the  cost of replacing this position at Step 1, Level V so the 
actual dollar figure savings are conservative with respect to actual current expenditures.  By 
converting to software hosted at Oakland Schools, we can avoid the costs of replacing this position 
when the incumbent retires, which is imminent within the next three years.   
IMPORTANT NOTE: Jill Valko, who is the current Media Technician, is a very important part of 
the media department and provides many services above and beyond maintenance of the sel f-hosted 
library automation system that will have to be reassigned to other staff upon her departure.  It will be a 
hardship on the department to not fill her position and I do not support making that cut.  However, 
economic realities dictate that I be pre pared for the likelihood that, upon her retirement, we will not be 
financially capable of staffing that position, and the cost benefit is clear in the table above.  On the 
other hand, we will not realize the full savings indicated until she does retire whi ch means that the 
actual one-time budget impact for the year of the conversion is an increase of $48,027 (includes cost 
avoidance of $2500 for not upgrading servers and redirecting the budgeted SirsiDynix annual 
maintenance to the conversion).  
To summarize, I recommend that we proceed with the Follett Destiny contract in the amount of 
$62,601, with overall budget impact as described above.  This item requires Board action due to the 
dollar amount of the contract; please place this on the April 20, 2010 age nda.  Thank you for your 
attention to this matter. 
  An Equal Opportunity/Affirmative Action Employer

MEMORANDUM 
To: Frank Lams, Purchasing Supervisor  
From: Scott Bryan, Director of Technology  
Date: April 1, 2010 
Re: Document camera purchase 
A component of the Smart Board project is new document cameras for the secondary schools.  We are able 
to integrate existing equipment in the elementary schools, th us do not need to purchase a new camera for 
each Smart Board classroom.  Of the 100 Smart Boards in this project, 30 will be going to secondary 
schools. 
Based upon past bids, we have discovered that competitive bids are unable to secure a lower price than  the 
REMC bid price of $995 each from City Animation.  Therefore I am requesting approval to purchase 30 
units from this vendor for the Smart Board project, for a total cost of $29,850.  
This item requires Board action due to the dollar amount of the purch ase; please place this on the April 20, 
2010 agenda.  Thank you for your attention to this matter.

  MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B -1200 
B-1208 VOTING 
 The Board of Education shall conduct all of its votes in   
 accordance with the applicable federal and state laws including, 
but not limited to, the Revised School Code and the Open 
Meetings Act, as amended. 
The roll call for voting is by random call of last names on a 
rotating basis. 
Adopted: January 16, 1979 
First Reading: March 16, 2010 
Second Reading: April 20, 2010 
119
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B -1200 
B-1211 PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
All meetings, except closed sessions, are open to the public.  The Board 
welcomes citizens’ comments.  The public participation portion of the 
meeting is limited to one-half hour, but exceptions may be made at the 
discretion of the Board president.   Each person will be allowed to 
speak for up to five minutes, except where the number of speakers 
exceeds the time limit.  In those instances, the President may reduce 
the five-minute limit to a three-minute limit for each speaker  or the 
president of the Board may waive the half –hour time limit.  In any 
event, no one’s right to address the Board will be denied.  
Each person wishing to address the Board of Education must identify 
himself or herself by name and address.  If the person is representing 
an organization or group, the person should indicate whether the 
comments or presentation represents the official view of the 
organization or group. 
Board of Education members may question speakers but are not 
obligated to answer questions or make statements or commitm ents in 
response to issues raised by the public.  In general, such issues will be 
referred to the Superintendent for investigation, study, and 
recommendation or designated as future agenda items for Board of 
Education consideration.  
Individuals addressing the Board should take into consideration the 
rules of common courtesy.  The public participation portion of the 
meeting cannot be used to make personal attacks against any 
individual school board member or school district employee that are 
totally unrelated to the manner in which the member or employee 
performs his or her duties. 
To ensure individual due process rights, the District maintains a 
formal process for handling complaints against individuals.  A 
problem involving an individual or specific incid ent is handled 
through administrative channels.  For assistance, contact the 
Superintendent’s office. 
The president or designee is responsible for the orderly conduct of the 
meeting and shall rule on such matters as to the appropriateness of the 
subject being presented.  
120
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B -1200 
B-1211 PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
The Board values public participation and recognizes the importance 
of public input on issues affecting the Board and the District.  This 
Policy has been adopted to balance the importance of public 
participation with other important object ives, including: insuring all 
community members a fair and reasonable opportunity to express 
their views to the Board; completing Board business effectively, 
efficiently and in an orderly manner; respecting other important time 
commitments of Board members , District staff and audience members; 
and, respecting the rights of Board members, District staff , students, 
and audience members. 
Public participation will be limited to 60 minutes per Board meeting.  
Generally, there will be 30 minutes of public partici pation at the 
beginning of the meeting, limited to agenda items.  There will be an 
additional 30 minutes of public participation at the end of the meeting, 
limited to non-agenda items. 
Although the public participation portion of the meeting is limited, an  
exception may be made, if necessary, so that no one’s right to address 
the Board will be denied.  
Audience members who wish to address the Board must complete a 
Speaker Request Form.  Forms will be available 30 minutes prior to the 
start of the meeting and  must be completed and submitted to the 
Secretary of the Board of Education before the start of the meeting.  
The Board President or presiding officer will recognize audience 
members for public participation subject to the following rules:  
 Each person wish ing to speak must identify self by name and 
address. 
 Recognized speakers may be permitted to speak for up to three (3) 
minutes.  However, the President of the Board may reduce the time 
limit for each speaker in order to accommodate all of the 
individuals who wish to address the Board of Education.  
 Recognized speakers may not transfer or delegate their time to 
others. 
121
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B -1200 
B-1211 PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
 Delegations who wish to express their views are encouraged to 
 select one speaker to present the delegation’s views to the  Board. 
 Each member  of a delegation wishing to speak must submit a 
 separate Speaker Request form.  
 Persons who have completed S peaker Request Forms but have not 
had an  opportunity to address the Board or to complete their 
presentations may be given the option of submitting t heir views in 
writing to the Secretary of the Board of Education, whose name and 
e-mail address are printed on each S peaker Request F orm.  The 
Secretary of the Board will forward all such written presentation to 
all Board members. 
 Speakers shall direct the ir comments to the Board and not to 
District staff or other audience members.  
 Speakers should not expect Board members to answer questions 
during public participation.  Questions will be referred to the 
Superintendent for review and study and, if appropria te, future 
Board action. 
 Speakers are expected to express themselves responsibly and 
courteously and with due respect for the fact their views and 
opinions may not be shared by all present.  The following are 
examples of irresponsible or discourteous expression:  
 Denigrating Board members, District staff , students, or other 
audience members. 
 Speculating on the motives of Board members, District staff ,  
students, or other audience members.  
 Complaints about District staff or students that have not been 
brought to the District’s attention through appropriate channels.  
 Speakers may not express complaints against Board members or 
District staff unrelated to their offices or jobs.  
122
MEETINGS OF THE BOARD     B -1200 
B-1211 PUBLIC PARTICIPATION IN BOARD OF EDUCATION 
MEETINGS 
 Speakers may not express complaints against Board members or 
District staff that are  related to their offices or job unless the 
affected person or persons have been notified and permitted a 
reasonable opportunity to choose whether to hold a hearing on the 
complaint in open or closed session.  
 Audience members are expected to conduct themse lves responsibly 
and courteously and with due respect for the fact their views and 
opinions may not be shared by all present.  Thus, raucous or 
disrespectful expressions of agreement or disagreement by 
audience members are not appropriate.  
 The Board’s Pres ident or presiding officer is charged with the 
responsibility for insuring that public participation assists the 
Board in discharging its responsibilities and is conducted according 
to this Policy.  Thus, the Board President or presiding officer is 
authorized to enforce this Policy by, among other things: 
interrupting presentations to remind speakers and audience 
members of this Policy; dismissing previously recognized speakers 
who violate this Policy; requesting speakers or audience members 
to leave the me eting if they violate the Policy and, if necessary, 
requesting the assistance of law enforcement officers to assist in 
removing speakers or audience members who refuse to leave; or, 
adjourning the meeting. 
The Board may amend or waive this Policy, or speci fic provisions 
thereof, when, in the Board’s judgment, amendment or waiver is 
appropriate. 
Revised: April 1988

First Rdg: March 16, 2010 
Second Rdg: April 20, 2010

   RULES AND REGULATIONS    C -1400 
C-1402 SMOKING 
The policy of the Troy School District is to follow Public Act 140 of 
1993 regarding smoking and use of tobacco products on school 
district property, with the exception that smoking is prohibited at 
athletic fields. 
It is the policy of the Troy School District that the use and/or sale of 
any tobacco products is prohibited in vehicles, buildings, facilities, 
structures, or other real estate owned, leased or otherwise 
controlled by the Troy School Distric t at all times (24 hours a day – 
7 days a week.) 
For the purpose of this policy, the use and/or sale of a tobacco 
product is defined as follows: 
“Tobacco product” means a preparation of tobacco to be inhaled, 
chewed, or placed in a person’s mouth.  
“Use of a tobacco product” means any of the following:  
 The carrying by a person of a lighted cigar, cigarette, pipe, or 
 other lighted smoking device.  
 The inhaling or chewing of a tobacco product.  
 The placing of a tobacco product within a person’s mouth.  
Adopted: February 2, 1999 
Revised: September, 2003 
First Rdg: March 16, 2010 
Second Rdg: April 20, 2010

   ACCOUNTING AND REPORTING     D -1100 
D-1106 FIXED ASSET POLICY  
The Board shall maintain a fixed asset procedure sufficient to 
track applicable purchases and donations of items meeting the 
following criteria: 
 The cost of each item shall exceed $5,00 0;  
 The item shall have an estimated useful life which exceeds 
one year; 
 Items costing less than $5,000 each may also be included 
within this policy if such item is deemed to be theft prone or 
otherwise warranting tracking;  
Fixed assets shall be classif ied into the following major 
categories: 
 Land 
 Buildings  
 Improvements other than buildings  
 Construction in Progress  
 Machinery and Equipment  
The Assistant Superintendent of Business Services shall develop 
such procedures and practices sufficient to meet t his policy. 
First Reading: March 16, 2010 
Second Reading:     April 20, 2010

ADVERTISING & DISTRIBUTION OF MATERIALS IN THE SCHOOLS  
K-1300  
K-1303 POLITICAL AND/OR COMMERCIAL ADVERTISING BY 
DISTRICT EMPLOYEES 
 No material supporting any candidate, candidates, slate of candidates, 
political issues, issues which cause substantial  and material interference 
and/or disruption with normal school activities , or political 
organizations/committees may be distributed, posted, or displayed on 
any district-owned property. 
 Except for the reserved length of time in a building or room which 
has been rented or reserved by a political or community group for 
meeting purposes or in connection with a class project or activities.  
All school personnel, including contracted personnel assigned to the  
school district, shall maintain a posture of compl ete neutrality with 
respect to all candidate(s), political issues, and/or political 
organizations/committees while engaged in duties for the Troy School 
District or in contact with students or students’ parents. 
Further, absent the express written consent  of the superintendent or 
his/her designee, all school personnel shall be restricted from utilizing 
Troy School District resources, technology , including District e-mail, and 
student/parent contact information received while engaged in duties for 
the Troy School District for commercial purposes or for the purpose of 
supporting any candidate, candidates, slate of candidates, political 
issues, including any issues which cause substantial and material 
interference and/or disruption with normal school activitie s, or political 
organizations/committees.  
Adopted: May 3, 1976 
Revised: January 16, 1979 
 April 21, 1998 
First Rdg: March 16, 2010 
Second Rdg: April 20, 2010 
127
ADVERTISING & DISTRIBUTION OF MATERIALS IN THE SCHOOLS  
K-1300  
K-1304 SERVICE ADVERTISING 
 Advertising of a service to employees or service to students may be allowed by 
obtaining approval of the superintendent or designee. 
 ADVERTISING ON SCHOOL DISTRICT PROPERTY AND IN SCHOOL 
DISTRICT ELECTRONIC MEDIA  
The school district property and electronic media, like school newspapers, 
yearbooks, and other District-sponsored publications and property, is a nonpublic 
forum subject to the District’s control and regulation.  The District will accept 
commercial advertisements, subject to the following rules and procedures. 
 Advertising is restricted to commercial purposes.  (The District reserves the 
right to give preference to local businesses and businesses that sell goods and 
services that students may be interested in purchasing.)  The District property 
and Web site are not forums for political, religious, or public advocacy 
discussion or expression. 
 The District reserves the right to deny advertisements that are ungrammatical, 
poorly written, inadequately researched, biased or prejudiced, vulgar or 
profane or otherwise unsuitable for student audiences. 
 Advertisements cannot be for goods or services which are illegal. 
 The sponsors, goods, services, advertisement or activities may not violate or 
condone (explicitly or implicitly) the violation of Board or School District’s 
policies, or be inconsistent with or in competition with the educational mission 
of the School District. 
 The advertisements cannot be libelous, offensive, contain explicit sexual 
content, or be obscene. 
 The District will not approve advertising in connection with the following 
products or uses: 
1. Products used to injure or kill. 
2. Alcohol-related products. 
3. Tobacco-related products. 
4. Illegal drug-related products. 
128
5. Prescription or over-the-counter medication. 
6. Sexually suggestive products or language. 
7. Gambling-related products. 
8. Products that present an unacceptable risk of liability. 
9. Products that are harmful to or inconsistent with the mission or image of 
the School District. 
10. Racist, sexist, hateful, demeaning or degrading language or statements. 
11. Profanity. 
12. Sexual acts. 
13. Statements impugning any school in the Troy School District or schools 
outside of the Troy School District. 
14. Art or a design incorporating trademarks or copyrights owned by third 
parties, unless written permission of such use is satisfactory in form and 
substance to the school, is obtained from the trademark holder or 
copyright owner, and approved in advance. 
15. Any use which suggests or implies the school district’s endorsement of 
other organizations, companies, products, services, political parties or 
views, or religious organizations or beliefs. 
16. Any use that discriminates or implies discrimination against any persons 
or groups based on age, ancestry, belief, color, creed, disability, national 
origin, race, ethnicity, religion, sex, sexual orientation or veteran status, or 
in any other way that would be a violation of the School District’s anti-
discrimination policies or practices. 
All advertisements are to be reviewed by the superintendent or designee who is 
responsible for applying and enforcing this policy.  Any entity or person who 
disagrees with the superintendent or designee’s decision can petition the 
superintendent’s office for a review within 30 calendar days from the initial 
written decision of the designee.  The superintendent, or the superintendent’s 
designee, will review the petition and upon request meet with the petitioner.  The 
superintendent or designee will issue a decision within 30 calendar days after the 
petition and/or meeting which decision shall be final. 
Adopted: April 19, 1976 
Revised: January 16, 1979 
 April 21, 1998 
 April 15, 2003 
First Rdg: March 16, 2010 
Second Rdg:  April 20, 2010
